### 2.  Violation‑Reasoning Prompt (for “violates”)

```
You are an expert in geospatial topological reasoning.

Vernacular-to-Topology Reference:
  WITHIN    — "is in", "located in", "part of"              (A is fully inside B)
  CONTAINS  — "home to", "includes", "hosts"                (A fully encloses B)
  TOUCHES   — "borders", "adjacent to", "across from" (water)  (shared boundary only)
  CROSSES   — "passes through", "along", "at the mouth of" (full LineString traversal)
  DISJOINT  — "far from", "miles away", "separate from"     (no shared points)
  OVERLAPS  — "partly in", "extends into", "county seat of" (partial interior overlap)
  EQUALS    — "identical perimeter", "same boundary"        (identical geometry)

Strong (unambiguous) signals for each predicate:
  within     : "is in", "located in", "part of", "within"
  contains   : "home to", "location of", "includes", "hosts"
  touches    : "shares a border", "adjacent to", "along", "at the mouth of", "across from" (water)
  crosses    : "passes through", "along", "at the mouth of" (full LineString traversal)
  disjoint   : "far from", "miles away", "separate from", "suburb of" (no contact)
  overlaps   : "partly in", "extends into", "county seat of", "shares some area"
  equals     : "identical perimeter", "same boundary", "coextensive", "municipal limits mirror county lines"

WARNING — ambiguous vernacular (read carefully):
  "suburb of" alone does NOT imply disjoint — the suburb may be adjacent or even overlapping.
  "along" + river/road → likely crosses (full LineString), not just touches.
  "same as" / "mirrors" → may be equals only if BOTH geometries are truly identical.
  "county seat of" alone does NOT imply within — the city may extend into adjacent counties → overlaps.

Rules (apply in order):
1.  DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before answering):  "contains" means A encloses B; "within" means A is inside B."
    • Ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A?
    • If the data shows B encloses A, the answer is "within", NOT "contains".
2.  VERNACULAR PRIORITY (override geometry when contradictory):
      - "most sides", "surrounded by" (partial encirclement) → touches
      - "partly in", "extends into", "part of population in", "county seat of" (partial interior overlap) → overlaps
      - "enclave of" → touches (shared boundary, not interior)
      - "across from" (water) → touches (shared water boundary)
      - "to the direction" / "from the direction" → NOT a topological relation (use for directional reasoning only)
  3.  ENTITY VALIDATION:  If a mismatch warning is flagged, treat that geometry as unreliable.
  4.  GEOMETRY TYPE CONSTRAINTS:
        • LineString (river, road) traversing Polygon interior → crosses
        • LineString touching only Polygon boundary → touches
        • "At the mouth of" / "upstream from" → check bbox and polygon; often crosses
        • Two Polygons partially sharing area → overlaps
  5.  BOUNDING BOX CAVEAT (never conclude directly from bbox):
        - bbox overlap does NOT imply polygon overlap
        - bbox containment does NOT imply within/contains
        - Treat bbox clues, not proof
  6.  GAP TOLERANCE:  A bbox gap of < 0.01 degrees (~1 km) is NOT evidence of disjoint.  Administrative boundaries often share exact edges with 0‑meter gap.
  7.  VERNACULAR PRIORITY (

  [CoT] ✅ FINAL PREDICTION: disjoint

RESULT | Expected=overlaps | Predicted=disjoint | WRONG | Acc=53.25%

### 2.  Prompt Engineering Strategy

| Prompt Component | Purpose | Example |
|-------------------|---------|---------|
| **Vernacular-to-Topology Reference** | Provide concise mapping rules for each predicate, including directional cues and common textual qualifiers. | *within* — "is in", "located in", "part of" (A is fully inside B) |
| **WARNING — ambiguous vernacular** | Highlight phrases that often mislead the model (e.g., "suburb of" → disjoint, not touches). | "county seat of" → does NOT imply within — the city may extend into adjacent counties → overlaps |
| **Rules** | Step‑by‑step checklist to avoid common mistakes. | 1. DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before answering): ... |
| **Entity A / Entity B** | Explicitly list the entities, their types, and coordinates. | Entity A: Texhoma, Texas (type: village, geometry: Polygon) |
| **Vernacular** | The raw sentence fragment with the two entities. | Vernacular: "Texhoma, Texas separate from Texhoma,Oklahoma" |
| **Valid predicates** | Narrow the model’s choice to the legal set. | Valid predicates: contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals |
| **OpenStreetMap (OSM) Evidence** | Provide high‑confidence geometric data (coordinates, bbox, hierarchy) for each entity. | Entity A: ... |
| **Bounding Box Analysis** | Use bbox overlap/gap as a *clue*, not proof. | Bboxes nearly adjacent (gap ≈ 0.007 km) — this small gap does NOT confirm disjoint. |
| **Think step‑by‑step** | Encourage the model to follow the evidence hierarchy and avoid single‑step reasoning. | 1. Check entity validation warnings — if the data is unreliable, do not trust it. 2. ... |
| **Final Decision Prompt** | Explicitly ask for the one correct predicate, referencing the vernacular. | Final Decision Prompt: The sentence says "Texhoma, Texas separate from Texhoma,Oklahoma". Which of the following predicates best captures this relationship? ... |

### 3.  Antidote to “Within” Bias

The vernacular is the strongest evidence. The phrase *“separate from”* is a classic disjoint cue. Do not rely on the OSM bbox or coordinates when the text is unambiguous.

**Antidote Prompt** (insert before the final decision):
> *The sentence explicitly says "separate from" — this is a definitive disjoint cue. The model often mis‑reads the OSM bbox as evidence for within, but the vernacular is clear. Do not guess; use the rules above.*>

### 4.  5‑Step Reasoning Checklist (run before answering)

| Step | Question | Verdict |
|------|----------|---------|
| 1 | **Equals** — Does the text say the two entities are identical or co‑extensive? | No |
| 2 | **Within** — Is A completely inside B? | No |
| 3 | **Contains** — Is B completely inside A? | No |
| 4 | **Overlaps** — Do A and B share some interior area but neither fully contains the other? | No |
| 5 | **Disjoint** — Are A and B completely separate, with no shared boundary or interior? | Yes |

### 5.  Final Prompt (use the template below)

```
